- Independent source reconciliation found **no demonstrated same-block fault cycle** and no direct user CUDA kernel tracker entries. The generic wait helper existed in stock R580; stock also performs other tracker waits under the VA-block lock. Keep patch `0002` unloaded until a controlled canary test and verified rollback path resolve it.

The latest PC3 preflight remains blocked by unsigned modules, unproven signing-key trust, and active device-major conflicts; resolve those in an isolated/reversible plan before any experimental load. Preserve the active PC3 host/VM driver stacks.

## Latest increment: R580 UVM external-allocation provider boundary (2026-10-23)

- Added [`audit_r580_uvm_semaphore_mapping_api.py`](../tools/audit_r580_uvm_semaphore_mapping_api.py) and seven focused regression tests. The audit pins the R580.178.04 archive (`060749a0df733af2d0bc863fff4581faca398048d17dbc691d039141ead391d8`), patch `0002` (`23145778c944ae2cf222a6df192d97a1b3260c564772f4b353d48f0cf0cb26f0`), seven NVIDIA source files and three hybrid bridge files. It reads no-follow regular-file snapshots, hashes and analyzes those same bytes, and rejects concurrent source changes. A regression also covers even-backslash C string escapes.
- Static result: the public R580 interface allocates/mutates system memory through an RM VA space, uses RM handles for external duplication, and declares no raw Linux page/DMA descriptor import. `UVM_MAP_EXTERNAL_ALLOCATION` captures `rmCtrlFd`, `hClient`, and `hMemory`; the inspected path stores the fd but does not pass it to `DupMemory`. The RM callback receives the target device plus client/memory handles. The hybrid bridge selects the target device's provider and forwards those handles without source-provider identity; subsequent mapping requires the VA-space and duplicated-memory owners to match. Report status is `no-source-owner-route-in-inspected-path`, limited to the pinned public R580 path and current bridge; this does not rule out private RM import mechanisms or other DMA-BUF paths. [Mode-0600 report](../evidence/r580-uvm-semaphore-mapping-api-v2-20261023T2235Z.json), SHA-256 `4f33ff89ded1bfd4a7724b5cc2e9c5348c1e5080a99858e9dbe5507d7dc7a654`; auditor SHA-256 `be36303a8dd39eed39b2d03c11b82cd1cc0b79b7a1bce5c39ebaf13405f0a29b`; tests SHA-256 `be666712bd6ecec6ccf4c4eb49759d51f064fac14c85eecc45ea0eb700aa5c45`.
- Focused audit tests pass **7/7**. Fresh full pinned suite passes **1,237 tests**, exit 0, in **56.861 s**; both CUDA probe variables resolve to `build/probe-system-pageable-memory-v1-20261023/hybrid_cuda_probe`, SHA-256 `6e4f93e20b970205de1516c1258bdea83bbd39a72ba3c4ef719f114569fd497b`. [Mode-0600 report](../evidence/software-verification-r580-uvm-semaphore-mapping-api-v2-20261023T2235Z.json), SHA-256 `f2ebc124594517e35c61274dae63f16c18c79b92467204c282c6aac63f5aab2b`; [log](../evidence/logs/software-verification-r580-uvm-semaphore-mapping-api-v2-20261023T2235Z.log), SHA-256 `626ca6a0442926b8c6461ac12a62590e0c0057996eb8843309af7daa3a586b0d`. Software/source/build checks only; no experimental module load, same-kernel hybrid CUDA, or UVM hardware validation.
- PC6 read-only monitor at `2026-10-23T22:01:19Z`: Claude Opus 5.5/max process remained alive, Ralph state still said iteration 1/12 (state mtime 21:28Z), output directory had no files, and no Sonnet child process was visible. The root-level `SONNET_REVIEW_ORDERING.md` identifies itself as a Sonnet 5.5 review; its SHA-256 is `e23fbb7f96f50eb7bfa764226ef6ecd811d5b956616c0b4ccf5f081d29065fa5`. It reports no runtime/hardware validation and identifies an assert-only cross-provider guard. This establishes the report’s content/hash, not that Sonnet actually ran. - **Next executable step:** trace how the hybrid frontend creates, exposes, and routes source RM client/memory handles, then evaluate a provider-aware allocation export/import boundary (including whether a documented DMA-BUF/RM import API can create a target-local RM handle). ## Latest increment: UVM CPU-wait lock hazard and branch-flavor recheck (2026-10-23)

- Added [`audit_r580_uvm_wait_lock_context.py`](../tools/audit_r580_uvm_wait_lock_context.py) and four mutation tests. The pinned R580.178.04 path shows the opt-in isolated mode waits on cross-GPU tracker entries in the generic push wrapper; `block_unmap_gpu()` reaches that wrapper with `&block->tracker` through `uvm_va_block_unmap()` while `va_block->lock` is asserted held. A fault needed to complete the waited-on GPU work could need the same lock. This is a concrete **progress/deadlock risk**, not a proven deadlock or runtime observation. The patch must remain unloaded until the wait is redesigned or a source-backed progress argument and fault stress test resolve it. [Mode-0600 report](../evidence/r580-uvm-wait-lock-context-v1-20261023T2146Z.json), SHA-256 `98335f9066aa2ca1d18777cd60f76c6337769c38c35cc87bf090d6c06ce40420`; auditor SHA-256 `503544e572ad6c23ceab6614e488d2d1e89938477ec7b8942abea144bb8a7f38`; tests SHA-256 `15f989a4403b2713ff197a2bdf6a0734a423d5a70f0dd21d0b8e36f217b3cec2`.
- The pinned complete software suite passes **1,230 tests**, zero failures/errors/skips, in **56.361 s**. Both CUDA probe variables are pinned to SHA-256 `6e4f93e20b970205de1516c1258bdea83bbd39a72ba3c4ef719f114569fd497b`. [Mode-0600 report](../evidence/software-verification-r580-uvm-isolated-execution-v7-20261023T2146Z.json), SHA-256 `69b3724dfd2f5a8e694c2cd2ae23058d6b07f475d55a0a5397a339da31f136ce`; [log](../evidence/logs/software-verification-r580-uvm-isolated-execution-v7-20261023T2146Z.log), SHA-256 `8e7d7f22a6d81f1b1454fc17405b65980f1a1b9ba1b99093f7128251366f551d`. Software/source/build suite only; no hybrid runtime/hardware validation.
- Rechecked NVIDIA's exact R615.78.08 module guide and open-module README: the flavors are mutually exclusive; proprietary R615 covers Turing through Hopper while Blackwell requires Open, and Open starts at Turing/GSP. Thus no official R615 module flavor spans Volta plus Blackwell. This confirms the flavor boundary behind the same-version R580 two-backend experiment; it does not validate co-loading or make the current CPU-wait patch safe. See [official R615 module guide](https://download.nvidia.com/XFree86/Linux-x86_64/615.78.08/README/kernel_open.html) and [R615.78.08 Open module README](https://github.com/NVIDIA/open-gpu-kernel-modules/blob/615.78.08/README.md).
- **Next executable step:** finish the call-site/lock review and choose a synchronization mechanism that does not sleep under UVM block locks—most likely a provider-local GPU-visible semaphore mapping, or a separately owned UVM design with explicit routing. Then implement a narrow source/CPU contract and failure-path tests. Refresh the PC3 preflight read-only after the design is safe. Current R580 modules are still compile-only, unsigned candidates remain blocked by the recorded preflight, and neither GPU has been used by the experimental module.

[Mode-0600 report](../evidence/software-verification-r580-uvm-isolated-execution-v4-rerun-20261023T2131Z.json), SHA-256 `d4cdb4c77ec54314f3d242cd3e2b1e7f1b1c91bf3fbbc12037359f057bcdccd8`; [log](../evidence/logs/software-verification-r580-uvm-isolated-execution-v4-rerun-20261023T2131Z.log), SHA-256 `5cb8d5a6a80df24652725039225da23a552242a192e301ee20e98f34eec1d066`. - The patch's new CPU wait closes the direct pageable-migration acquire path; the direct-call census now confirms all three direct acquire sites are guarded or safe under the patch. The opt-in patch skips cross-provider semaphore-pool duplication and gives each semaphore page CPU plus owner-GPU mappings. This does not establish shared managed memory, cross-provider VA mappings, P2P, or same-process hybrid CUDA. The follow-up source audit now checks the owner mapping, CPU polling chain, and teardown separately.
- The latest full suite and owner-mapping audit are recorded in the increment above. This increment remains compile/static/CPU-test evidence; no module installation, insertion, VM change, or hardware execution occurred.

## Latest increment: UVM owner isolation architecture audit (2026-10-23)

- Added [`audit_uvm_owner_isolation_architecture.py`](../tools/audit_uvm_owner_isolation_architecture.py) with three fail-closed tests. Hash-pinned R580.178.04 source confirms one `nvidia-uvm.ko` target, one `nvidia-uvm` device identity, a module-global GPU table and RM session, and UVM fd checks tied to that module's `file_operations`. The current hybrid bridge has one callback registry, rejects a second registration while active, and turns one logical UVM session into a paired Open/legacy session.
- **Architecture choice for the first experiment:** one UVM control plane plus a provider-local map for the same system-memory semaphore page. This preserves the existing UVM/API path and is a smaller experiment than multiplying UVM ioctl/session owners. The new map still needs explicit DMA/IOMMU, visibility/order, invalidation and lifetime proof; no mapping implementation or runtime result is claimed. Isolated UVM owners remain technically possible, but require distinct module/device identities, independent callback/session routing, a single-process CUDA-to-UVM-fd router, and explicit cross-owner memory semantics.

## Latest increment: pinned R580 UVM admission blocker and canary gate (2026-10-23)

- Added [`audit_r580_uvm_cross_provider.py`](../tools/audit_r580_uvm_cross_provider.py) and four focused tests. The audit pins the R580.178.04 source archive (`060749a0df733af2d0bc863fff4581faca398048d17dbc691d039141ead391d8`) plus the exact UVM and bridge source hashes. It traces channel-pool creation to tracking-semaphore pages, then traces second-GPU initialization through page mapping and `nvUvmInterfaceDupAllocation`.
- **Static finding C1:** in non-Confidential-Computing mode, when an existing initialized GPU has a semaphore page, adding a GPU owned by the other RM provider reaches the bridge's cross-provider rejection (`NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED`) before either RM is called. R580 UVM propagates this error and aborts the second GPU's initialization before creating its channel manager. This disproves the older statement that returning P2P `NONE` lets mixed-provider discovery continue. It does not prove a runtime failure on hardware or that a new common-memory mapping contract is impossible. [Mode-0600 audit](../evidence/r580-uvm-cross-provider-static-v2-20261023T204103Z.json), SHA-256 `a02b4d8b9e1498ba1aab379121ebb1f38c206bf5942d035ab96af98aaf63feb9`.

## Previous increment: cdev lifetime fix, module-owner audit, and R580 flavor confirmation (2026-10-23)

- Fixed the character-device lifetime pin: both frontend `struct cdev` objects now copy `fops->owner` before `cdev_add()`. Linux `cdev_get()` uses `cdev->owner` before opening the file, and `cdev_init()` does not initialize that owner. The regression was observed RED before the source fix and GREEN afterward; focused standard-node/frontend tests pass.
- Rebuilt the R580 frontend against Linux `7.0.0-38-generic`: module compiled with 291 imports resolved, zero unresolved, objtool active and no warnings. Module SHA-256 `b47e2fc33dc217b98015234438229acc5958899f9355a14209b3eac52a649cd6`; [mode-0600 build report](../evidence/hybrid-frontend-r580-cdev-owner-v1-20261023T185925Z.json), SHA-256 `bd4263bfe66030c1912ad54940396a374901184607114fa79feb49bf22d4f6de`. It was not installed or loaded; no RM or GPU operation occurred.
- The refreshed full CPU/software suite passed **1,155 tests**, zero failures/errors/skips, in **57.115 s**. [Mode-0600 report](../evidence/software-verification-symbol-owner-cdev-v1-20261023.json), SHA-256 `706f959c9f6847c45271b87900c30966cc1164d63c3bbd708ca2c3fc5c9dc77e`; [mode-0600 log](../evidence/logs/software-verification-symbol-owner-cdev-v1-20261023.log), SHA-256 `302867677d42311bfa843e5816ede1c9f8c78ce8094b1c4e9c69aed308d2d1ed`. Qualification is software-only; no experimental module is loaded/installed and runtime/hardware validation remains false.
- Extended the UVM symbol-coverage auditor with an order-sensitive global-export check. The real R580 UVM module has 76 imports, all present with matching CRCs in the intended R580 frontend. The official R595 `nvidia` export table overlaps 74 of these: 70 have the same CRC and 4 differ (`GetP2PCaps`, `GetPmaObject`, `QueryCopyEnginesCaps`, `RetainChannel`). Linux v7.0 `find_symbol()` searches the kernel then the loaded module list and returns the first matching export; the selected provider's CRC is checked afterward. Thus equal CRC does not pin the intended owner, while a different earlier CRC can reject an import. **Actual load order and binding were not observed.** This corrects the earlier assumption that duplicate exported names alone necessarily make module insertion fail. [Mode-0600 comparison report](../evidence/r580-uvm-r595-global-symbol-precedence-v1-20261023.json), SHA-256 `442470ed154f37b52e9de184ab25d8618f05b91f4f6347b4812518bbfb142284`.
- Official NVIDIA R580.178.04 materials confirm the package lists Tesla V100, RTX 5080 and RTX 5090, while defining mutually exclusive kernel-module flavors: proprietary supports Volta through pre-Blackwell; Open supports Blackwell and requires Turing-or-later GSP. Both flavors use identical user-space components. This supports **Architecture C using same-version R580 user space plus isolated proprietary-R580 and Open-R580 backend modules** as the first hybrid prototype. It does not prove one Linux kernel, one process, or the custom frontend works. R595 UVM co-load is no longer the primary integration path; it remains a source of candidate improvements after compatibility review. R580 Open-only HMM/GDS and fault-handler features do not transfer to a pre-Turing V100 on the proprietary path.
- **Next executable step:** qualify the same-version R580 backend pair and the common Driver API routing boundary statically, including module names, symbol ownership, standard char-device ownership, and one UVM owner. Keep the experimental load behind a reversible canary and explicit system-change authorization. The full same-process V100+Blackwell CUDA criterion remains unvalidated.

The corrected v2 used the complete `/usr/src/nvidia-595.99.02` source root; the installed R595 UVM subtree and v39 source UVM subtree were byte-identical across 215 files. The v1 failure is retained as diagnostic evidence and is not treated as a driver incompatibility.

- Added the isolated `system-pageable-memory` CUDA Driver API phase. ## Latest increment: NVKMS-safe standard NVIDIA node registration (2026-10-23)

- Source inspection found that reserving all 256 minors under NVIDIA major 195 would collide with NVKMS, which owns `195:254`. `src/hybrid580-standard-node-devices.c` now reserves GPU minors `195:0–247` and control minor `195:255` as separate regions; cleanup releases both regions and removes devices/cdevs in reverse acquisition order. Pinned R580 headers define the same ranges, and pinned `nvidia-modeset-linux.c` reserves exactly `195:254`.
- Expanded the CPU/UBSan fixture to simulate NVKMS owning `195:254` and cover ten registration/lifecycle cases: success, both region-reservation failures, both cdev-add failures, class creation failure, the first and second GPU-node failures, control-node creation failure, and catalog overflow. It checks exact region/cdev/device cleanup, including reverse-order removal of GPU nodes. Focused test: **1/1 passed**.
- Independent review confirmed the range split and failure cleanup. It first compared the source against an older 2026-10-22 frontend build report; that was the wrong artifact. The current [2026-10-23 build report](../evidence/frontend-standard-node-modeset-v4-20261023.json) records the exact current source SHA-256 `58026dbeca9e5da8f77817370a83795abb81245aa69dfd1500a30c0f346c6b1e`; the existing linked module SHA-256 is `0b90a65940a9354cbda47568a80eeaf5fbe269dae70d60e50305783408aeab42`. Kbuild passed for kernel `7.0.0-38-generic`.
- Fresh complete software suite: **1,042 tests passed, zero failures/errors/skips, 47.071 s**. Both CUDA probe variables were pinned to executable SHA-256 `a35740f5b7bf625b05a03a33b8697b9e733986a68904ea693fbf7375690a3808`. [Mode-0600 report](../evidence/software-verification-standard-node-modeset-v3-20261023T1321Z.json), SHA-256 `5dae10af75d3963ff2cfd669125a627bc247c3d7a53d98da12c46edd09b486a3`; [mode-0600 log](../evidence/logs/software-verification-standard-node-modeset-v3-20261023T1321Z.log), SHA-256 `fcfb14b13ef52f76b48d437a59a9fbb039917a8c618975a9fd55dd039b6fc6d8`.
- This is source/CPU/UBSan/Kbuild evidence. The module remains unloaded and uninstalled; no VFS open, real RM request, CUDA enumeration, or GPU runtime was exercised. Standard `CARD_INFO`/typed routes compile, but `libcuda` acceptance and the complete CUDA control surface remain unproven. Same-Linux, same-process V100+Blackwell CUDA remains unvalidated.
- **Next executable step:** capture the stock RTX5080 `libcuda` file-open and descriptor lifecycle around a bounded `cuInit`/enumeration probe, with raw trace private and syscall arguments redacted from the structured report. Then use the observed first unsupported control to extend only the typed frontend path it requires. Do not use a module-load or driver-replacement experiment until the existing PC3 recovery/canary gate is satisfied.

## Previous increment: R580/R615 UVM audit v3 and dispatch blocker (2026-10-23)

- Reworked [`audit_volta_uvm_capability_map.py`](../tools/audit_volta_uvm_capability_map.py) after the independent Opus v2 critique. The static audit now parses both HAL class tables and the R615 selector: R580 contains all six required GV100 targets, R615 contains none, and each missing lookup returns `NV_ERR_INVALID_CLASS` before installing a HAL pointer. Four identical R580/R615 call sites are reported explicitly, with a warning that shared caller text does not establish matching callback semantics. The scanner masks comments/literals, handles LF/CRLF and GCC whitespace line splices, preserves physical LF line numbers, and declares that it does not preprocess macros.
- The PLC finding is now a **conditional** high-priority failure-atomicity risk. R580 checks Volta's unsupported `EnabledNoPlc` mode at line 861 before overlap unmap at 870. In R615, external-map code removes an overlap before the later RM PTE request carries `compressionType`; if proprietary RM rejects that mode on GV100, the previous mapping may already be gone. RM rejection on GV100 is unknown. The report does not claim a reproduced hardware failure.

Source inspection shows GV100 supplies its own Volta `init_properties` callback and table initialization copies parent operations only into null child slots. This justifies those parent stubs for this compile probe, not runtime safety. The 128 KiB Volta callback still returns `NULL` in the pinned R580 source.

- The focused UVM overlay/dispatch tests pass **20/20**. The full pinned repository suite passes **987 tests, zero failures/errors/skips, 46.468 s** with both CUDA probe variables pinned to executable SHA-256 `a35740f5b7bf625b05a03a33b8697b9e733986a68904ea693fbf7375690a3808`. ## Latest increment: R580/R595 UVM PMA allocation ABI and access census (2026-10-23)

- Extended the pinned-header memory audit through the UVM PMA allocation boundary. `nvUvmInterfacePmaAllocPages()` has the same signature in R580.178.04 and R595.99.02. `UvmPmaAllocationOptions` is 56 bytes in both, with matching offsets for all eight fields. The UVM consumers explicitly access the same five fields: `flags`, `numPagesAllocated`, `physBegin`, `physEnd`, and `resultFlags`; `minimumSpeed`, `regionId`, and `alignment` are not explicitly accessed in either pinned UVM source.
- The eight PMA allocation/result bits used by these sources have the same values across branches: `ALLOW_PARTIAL=4096`, `CONTIGUOUS=64`, `DONT_EVICT=1`, `NO_ZERO=1024`, `PINNED=2`, `PROTECTED_REGION=256`, `RESULT_IS_ZERO=1`, `SPECIFY_ADDRESS_RANGE=8`. Audit v5 hashes the allocation providers' UVM consumers and fails on changes to the measured layouts, declaration, field census, or flag values. [Report](../evidence/hybrid-uvm-branch-abi-audit-v5-20261023.json), mode 0600, SHA-256 `d2295a9e6ab0bd85bc220d1121c23c61ec3004900b211b190832f5e7745f5789`.
- Focused ABI tests passed **2/2**. Fresh pinned full software suite passed **919 tests, zero failures, 48.259 s**. [Suite report](../evidence/software-verification-uvm-pma-abi-v1-20261023T082744Z.json), mode 0600, SHA-256 `3ee00354e9080546a2a63d8742e522bfd829c39fed228d45d4a36a9a1b051883`; [full log](../evidence/logs/software-verification-uvm-pma-abi-v1-20261023T082744Z.log), mode 0600, SHA-256 `9891f866216162e2114b99cf075fa79b97790c6d8381cd18eeb7d63f36330530`.
- This closes the header/layout and UVM field-access comparison only. It does not establish the proprietary allocator's semantics or return values, PMA eviction/zeroing behavior, or hardware memory allocation. No module was installed or loaded and no GPU was used.

## Latest increment: R580/R595 framebuffer-info ABI and consumer closure (2026-10-23)

## Latest increment: standard GPU registration rejects raced quarantine (2026-10-22)

- A targeted concurrency review found that `hybrid_front_gpu_register_control_fd()` read `control->quarantined` before acquiring `transaction_lock`. If an ambiguous control operation quarantined that same session while registration waited for the lock, registration could still call the provider. The read outside the lock was also unsynchronized with the lock-protected writers.
- Added a deterministic threaded CPU/VFS-double regression scenario: hold the control transaction lock, start standard GPU `REGISTER_FD`, wait until it reaches the lock, quarantine the control under the lock, then release it. Before the fix the scenario failed because the provider callback ran; after the fix it returns `-EXDEV`, makes no backend call, restores the GPU association to `STANDARD_UNREGISTERED`, and successfully registers on retry after the control is made healthy. The fixture uses pthread synchronization and UBSan in the focused frontend suite; this models the interleaving, not actual Linux VFS behavior.
- `src/hybrid_frontend.c` no longer reads the quarantine flag before locking. It rechecks under `transaction_lock` before any provider side effect; a raced quarantine unlocks and resets the GPU association to the retryable unregistered state. Source SHA-256 `a0d4dc9b35839205e4b24330abc387ae071ff867caedf37ed4a8d3438190a4d6`; [test source](../tests/test_hybrid_frontend.py) SHA-256 `355a58a929d7ce1cb61b2e7cdec3f10a83a5be77d932428b56827900ef4cf8ad`.
- Focused `test_hybrid_frontend` plus `test_hybrid_frontend_gpu`: **55 tests passed**. Fresh pinned full suite: **862 tests passed, zero failures/errors/skips, 46.420 s**, with both probe variables pinned to SHA-256 `a35740f5b7bf625b05a03a33b8697b9e733986a68904ea693fbf7375690a3808`. ## Latest increment: standard GPU `REGISTER_FD` direct/XFER parity (2026-10-22)

- Pinned Open R580.178.04 `kernel-open/nvidia/nv.c:2503-2550` copies the 16-byte `nv_ioctl_xfer_t`, unwraps `cmd/size/ptr`, copies the selected payload, then dispatches it through the ordinary GPU ioctl path. The native `NV_ESC_REGISTER_FD` case in `escape.c` accepts the exact 4-byte `ctl_fd` payload. Therefore a small `REGISTER_FD` inside XFER is accepted by the native wrapper even though the SDK comment describes XFER as intended for oversized ioctl payloads.
- Fixed `src/hybrid_frontend.c` so standard GPU-file direct and XFER forms share one typed control-registration route. XFER copies its envelope and nested request to kernel locals before resolving and pinning the frontend control file, validates envelope direction/size and inner payload size, checks same-BDF/backend ownership, then calls the provider with the exact pinned control session. Other XFER commands retain the existing route; an unregistered GPU remains gated. This closes the generic-provider path that otherwise interpreted a frontend fd in the provider's fd table and avoids using mutable nested user memory after validation.

## Latest increment: same-invocation R580 lookup return-site correlation (2026-10-22)

- Independent review of the exact Linux 6.8 x86-64 Kprobe emulation supports a narrow discriminator: at callee entry, the saved stack begins with the emulated `CALL` continuation. The prototype resolves the pinned `_nv031364rm+0x92` site with a disabled, temporary Kprobe, computes its five-byte continuation privately, unregisters the resolver before the workload, and compares one no-fault stack word at `_nv031399rm` entry. It exports only counters. Static source basis: [Kprobes core](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.8/arch/x86/kernel/kprobes/core.c) and [x86 text patching](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.8/arch/x86/include/asm/text-patching.h). ## Latest increment: same-device R580 selector reuse (2026-10-22)

- Added a hash-pinned audit that joins the R580 RM object to the proprietary Linux wrapper and header sources. PCI probe allocates one `nv_linux_state_t`, derives `nv_state` using `NV_STATE_PTR`, calls `rm_init_private_state`, and publishes the same per-device container. The first character-device open looks up that published `nvl`, derives `nv_state` with the same macro, and reaches `rm_init_adapter` through `nv_start_device`.
- Both binary paths use `nv_state+0x274`: `_nv000794rm` during private-state init when flag bit `0x10` is set; `_nv000790rm` later during adapter init when the separate bit-3 gate is clear, copying the value into record `+0x12c8` before reaching `_nv031399rm`. The key normalization and other two zero key words match. An earlier lookup **could** seed a later hit only if the field value and shared registry entry remain unchanged.
- A platform-display probe reads `nvidia,disp-sw-soc-chip-id` into the member after its own private-state call, so this field can change on that route. The static audit does not show that route applies to the PCI V100 or establish runtime selector equality, gate values, cache insertion, or a hit. These remain unknown.
- Added [audit tool](../tools/audit_r580_same_device_selector_reuse.py), SHA-256 `9bb197be6dbd9fb081b603b3a267eb685bcd6c5fc426c4b0249249781fcfa982`, and [two tests](../tests/test_r580_same_device_selector_reuse.py), SHA-256 `e7d98c2568d5218d94dd5e881d93f57532f00f17d10b69b99bfa0664401a00cd`; **2/2 focused tests pass**. ## Previous increment: module-scoped R580 descriptor registry (2026-10-22)

- The new hash-pinned audit traces the R580.178.04 manager pointer `_nv029075rm` from the module-level `rm_init_rm()`/`rm_shutdown_rm()` path to per-device `rm_init_private_state()` and `rm_init_adapter()` lookups. The 8-byte BSS pointer is set by the manager constructor and cleared on initialization failure/teardown. `_nv031399rm` reaches one list at manager `+0x1e0`, guarded by the lock at registry `+0x950`; the list head is `+0x38`, and descriptors are matched on four words at `+0x88/+0x8c/+0x90/+0x94`. ## Previous increment: R580 pre-ISR MMU-fault guard analysis (2026-10-22)

- Static source and binary analysis now resolves the normal initialization pointer identity: `_nv049556rm` writes the RM child through `nv_state.priv[0]` and records `nv_state_t` at child `+0x9d8`; the successful `_nv000790rm` path later follows that backpointer and sets bit 3 at the same `priv+0x48` field. This supersedes the prior checkpoint's unresolved writer/identity statement.
- The wrapper registers `nvidia_isr` at `nv.c:1416` before bottom-half queue setup and `rm_init_adapter()` (`:1444-1468`). `nvidia_isr` calls `rm_gpu_handle_mmu_faults()` at `:2824` before UVM and `rm_isr()` (`:2841-2846`). The R580 proprietary object checks `nv_state`, `priv`, and `priv[0]` for null on the MMU helper path, but no check of bit 3 was observed before its RM helper calls. `rm_isr()` has a separate bit-3 and child-pointer guard; it cannot establish safety of the earlier call.
- The Linux wrapper has a second conditional pre-init path: on a serviceable-fault result with no RM-requested threaded bottom half, `nvidia_isr()` schedules `bottom_half_q_item` (`nv.c:2917-2918`). The item is initialized at `:1447`, directly before the queue’s list, lock, and semaphore initialization at `:1448`/`nv-kthread-q.c:215-221`; the scheduler itself enters `_raw_q_schedule()` after only an exit-state check. This gap is a possible use-before-init only if an IRQ and the fault condition arrive there. Source masking and runtime reachability are not established.
- These are static initialization-window risks, **not reproduced races or hardware defects**. GPU source-mask state, interrupt reachability, pending-fault state, queue-schedule behavior on the target kernel configuration, and the callees' behavior on partially initialized child state remain unknown. Keep frontend `drain_irqs` fail-closed at `-EOPNOTSUPP`.
- Structured evidence: [identity and pre-ISR report](../evidence/rea-r580-private-state-identity-v2-20261022.json), SHA-256 `b7da9ef16f7deb0d9a4a30a5cb9207964293d8b60277a896329cf124f540fddf`; 15/15 source, object, and log hashes validated in [verification log](../evidence/logs/verify-rea-r580-private-state-identity-v2-20261022.log), SHA-256 `42c5bb7c6a5696440ea9c49f4a5a8103d81d778871ea062918de81e41f9729f2`, mode 0600. Focused helper disassembly [log](../evidence/logs/rea-r580-pre-isr-helper-disasm-v1-20261022.log), SHA-256 `60b3ab2ece5541fa2ec7650370812cca76c298fd75f786a114a54ecf65bdcf61`, mode 0600. The proprietary Ghidra input is `proprietary580-wrapper-baseline-v1/.../nv-kernel.o_binary`, SHA-256 `5269a23f...6f4b`. The Open source snapshot's object is different (SHA-256 `5e9219a4...6bb8a`) and was not used as that proprietary analysis input.
- PC6 Claude audit remains separate: at `03:42:37Z` Ralph was active at iteration 1/8, Opus was busy, and `output/AUDIT.md` was absent. The transcript reported model metadata `claude-opus-5-5`; both Sonnet agent calls had returned nonempty results labeled `sonnet`, without a precise Sonnet version. Codex monitor performed a read-only check; none of this is an audit completion.
- No driver/provider code, module, binding, VM, firmware, or GPU state changed. No hardware test or full test-suite run was performed in this increment.
- **Next executable task:** inspect where R580 masks, acknowledges, or enables device interrupt sources around `rm_init_adapter()` and the queue initialization gap; establish whether an early serviceable fault can occur, then inspect only MMU helper callees needed to determine which child fields are consumed before readiness. Then encode the resulting ordering contract in a CPU test before considering a canary IRQ test.

## Previous increment: R580 IRQ and deferred-queue lifetime audit (2026-10-22)

- The comparative [entrypoint audit v5](../evidence/r580-pm-entrypoint-lifetime-v5-20261022.json), SHA-256 `fe11a76d316e9a615543014d6056b7bf91ba6169cc203810c35351721d0bb49c`, pins eight sources each from Open and proprietary R580.178.04; tool SHA-256 `d54e0a242cd32208175cb4b263391b3f1bdcb917a3597802c9eaa44000c0d61b`. Both register shareable legacy IRQs and threaded MSI-X handlers; their ISR can schedule `bottom_half_q`, while PM contains no explicit Linux IRQ synchronization or teardown.
- Bounded Ghidra analysis of `rm_disable_adapter()` and selected callees completed 11/11 internal-function decompilations on the pinned R580.178.04 object. It shows two `os_flush_work_queue()` calls and internal RM disable callbacks; the open wrapper source maps that call to an RM `nvk` queue, not `nvl->bottom_half_q`. The indirect GPU callbacks and hardware IRQ semantics remain unresolved, so this is not an ISR-drain proof. See [report](../evidence/rea-private-r580-rm-disable-quiescence-v1-20261022.json), SHA-256 `d64a35bdcfcc74995bb5d31ae076d6abdc45fe76cc91df2e503f1a1eec623d74`, and mode-0600 logs.
- Both R580 `nv_kthread_q_stop()` implementations flush the queue before setting `main_loop_should_exit`. `nv_kthread_q_schedule_q_item()` checks that flag before `_raw_q_schedule()`, whose queue-lock critical section does not recheck it. Adapter shutdown stops the ISR queue before releasing IRQ handlers. This exposes a source-level post-flush enqueue interleaving if a producer is still active; it is not a reproduced hardware failure, and the opaque `rm_disable_adapter()` may quiesce some producers. Do not claim otherwise until its exact behavior and lock ordering are established.
- `tests/test_nvidia_pm_entrypoints.py` now has seven focused tests, including mutations for shared-IRQ flags and teardown order plus a synthetic locked queue gate. The audit recognizes the candidate synchronization shape; it does not modify NVIDIA sources. CPU/source/build tests only; no hybrid GPU qualification.

- An independent mutation review found that C trigraph conversion and bare-CR physical lines were not modeled. The v9 auditor now converts all nine C trigraphs before backslash-newline splicing, normalizes CRLF and bare CR while retaining raw source offsets, then strips comments and rejects preprocessor directives in the procfs functions. Mutation tests cover a trigraph-created splice hiding a macro override, a bare-CR macro override, all trigraph mappings, and raw-line mapping after newline normalization. The exact depth-writer success tail and the previously covered directive/error-return cases remain constrained.

The report contains 39 true facts and 35 raw-source line entries. Earlier v7 and v8 reports remain historical; v8 was superseded after review found the trigraph and bare-CR gaps.
- The source-backed PM conclusion is unchanged: Linux `nvidia_isr()` copies RM faults before UVM top-half entry; `intrSetIntrEn_IMPL` changes only RM software state, and GV100's copier returns early only when a later caller observes that gate. This does not join an ISR already past the check. The NVIDIA PM wrappers inspected still contain no `synchronize_irq`, `disable_irq`, or `free_irq`; adapter shutdown has `free_irq`, outside PM.
- Additional source inspection found separate hardware-control paths, but did not qualify one for early suspend use: `intrSetIntrEnInHw_HAL` dispatches to a generated no-op for ChipHal values outside its listed Turing set; `osDisableInterrupts()` separately calls `intrSetStall_HAL`; normal non-Tegra dispatch assigns `intrSetStall_TU102`; and GPU state unload reaches `intrStateUnload_TU102`, which clears top and leaf enables. These findings are static source only. Runtime HAL selection, Volta register behavior, restore ordering, and whether this joins an in-flight RM copier remain unknown. Do not insert the helper into PM based on these observations alone.

The `rm_gpu_copy_mmu_faults` path registers per-device bookkeeping, invokes an indirect function-table callback, then performs cleanup. Its observed `os_acquire_spinlock`/`os_release_spinlock` pair protects the bookkeeping table update and ends before the callback; that lock does not join an in-flight Linux ISR. `rm_isr_bh` dispatches through unresolved per-object function pointers, so their locks and runtime HAL targets remain unknown. See [private ISR-order report](../evidence/rea-private-r580-isr-order-v1-20261022.json) and pinned mode-0600 logs. No original source was recovered or changed.

It found that a list lock does not pin IRQ lifetime, `NV_FLAG_OPEN` is set only after IRQ registration, close frees vectors, PCI remove bypasses `nv_system_pm_lock`, and `synchronize_irq()` alone does not flush deferred NVIDIA work or prevent a later interrupt. ## Previous increment: R580 IRQ/PM ordering audit (2026-10-21)

- Extended the pinned-source audit and tests to cover Linux IRQ registration, MSI-X serialization, the RM interrupt-state gate, and PM depth. The exact R580.178.04 source had 18 hashed input files and **36 passing static predicates** in [`fault-service audit v5`](../evidence/r580-fault-interrupt-service-v5-20261021.json), SHA-256 `f7bb827279c131cd32bb05476e844daebd6c858758280290635bc17b9052afd6`, mode 0600.
- `nvidia_isr()` copies RM faults before entering the UVM top half. Normal IRQ registration points directly at this ISR; MSI-X uses a wrapper that serializes MSI-X calls, but the PM path does not take that lock. Hibernate/standby stores `INTERRUPT_TYPE_DISABLED` before the GPU power transition; `intrSetIntrEn_IMPL` updates RM software state, and the GV100 copier returns early when it observes that state. This blocks a later copier that sees the gate but does not join an ISR already past the check. The selected NVIDIA PM wrappers contain no `synchronize_irq`, `disable_irq`, or `free_irq`; adapter shutdown has `free_irq`, outside PM.
- Scope correction from independent review: the preempt → UVM suspend → per-device suspend order applies only to the successful full-depth procfs path. MODESET depth returns before preemption/UVM; UVM depth returns after UVM and before per-device suspend. Linux PCI `.suspend` calls `nvidia_suspend()` directly and does not call the global UVM suspend wrapper. These distinctions are source predicates and adversarially tested.
- The generic Linux source at upstream commit `028ef9c96e96197026887c0f092424679298aae8` runs `suspend_device_irqs()` in the later DPM noirq phase after normal device callbacks; its IRQ PM code synchronizes relevant IRQs. This is later than the NVIDIA `.suspend` callback and is not verified against the exact Ubuntu `7.0.0-38-generic` source on PC3. It cannot by itself prove that a prior procfs UVM drain excluded subsequent RM copies or that those copies were consumed. This is source reasoning, not an observed failure.
- The v5 increment passed 652 software tests. Keep UVM drain patch `0001` unqualified; do not add a blanket shared-IRQ disable or claim in-flight ISR completion without proving normal/MSI-X vectors, RM producer gates, and restore ordering. No GPU or PM operation ran.
- The previous PC6 check found `claude` unavailable on the target host; this supersedes older notes that described it as installed but unauthenticated.

## Previous increment: frontend PM admission primitive (2026-10-21)

- Added `src/hybrid580_frontend_admission.h`, a lock-serialized gate shared by the Linux frontend and a pthread/UBSan contract fixture. The frontend now counts `.open`, `.unlocked_ioctl`, and final `.release`; sleepable entries wait in a freezable state while blocked. `.mmap` attempts entry without sleeping and returns `-EAGAIN` while blocked because the VFS callback cannot park while holding the caller's mmap lock. `.poll` registers a global gate waitqueue and reports no readiness while closed. A block operation closes new admission, `drain` waits for current operations to leave, and `unblock` wakes entrants/pollers.
- CPU coverage exercises active-operation draining, waiters remaining asleep until unblock, leave underflow rejection, gated poll/mmap behavior, epoll gate-wait registration, and the frontend file-operation wiring. The first harness run exposed two fixture omissions (Blackwell BDF inventory and epoll gate-qproc accounting); both fixtures were corrected, after which the targeted suite passed 58 tests in 0.978 s. ## Previous increment: per-GPU channels-only PM control (2026-10-21)

- Added provider exports `pm_channels_suspend_v1(generation)` and `pm_channels_resume_v1(generation)` to both private R580 backends. They use the provider device-list lock, validate that every device belongs to the armed recovery generation, and invoke only `nv_preempt_user_channels()` / `nv_restore_user_channels()`; PCI DPM retains GPU power and per-GPU modeset ownership.
- Added a separate per-GPU channel generation alongside the DPM callback recovery latch. The provider marks each GPU before attempting preemption, so a partial failure remains discoverable; successful restores clear only that GPU's latch and failed restores remain retryable. DPM callback results can be retired before UVM resumes, while the independent channel generation stays active until every GPU's channels are restored; a new recovery cycle is rejected while old channel state remains. This separation corrects the first draft, which incorrectly coupled channel state to `recovery_end` and did not permit the normal DPM/UVM/channel-resume order.
- Changed the CPU coordinator to record a backend as attempted before invoking channel suspend. Rollback therefore calls restore for the backend whose suspend returned an error as well as prior successful backends. A restore failure keeps that backend marked and the frontend quarantined. Added tests for partial suspend cleanup and per-GPU restore retry.

## Previous increment: channels-only PM defers NVKMS to PCI DPM (2026-10-21)

- The contradicted R580 sequence was real in source: the aggregate `CHANNELS` depth called `nvidia_modeset_suspend(0)`/`resume(0)`, while later per-GPU DPM callbacks also entered NVKMS for logical GPU 0. If channel preemption failed, rollback additionally called an unmatched global NVKMS resume. Since `nvkms_suspend(0)` holds its global write semaphore until `nvkms_resume(0)`, these paths risked a second lock acquisition or an unpaired unlock.
- Added Open and proprietary patch `0036-private-*-pm-channel-dpm-modeset.patch`. Aggregate suspend, aggregate resume, and failed-suspend rollback now skip GPU-zero NVKMS dispatch at `NV_PM_ACTION_DEPTH_CHANNELS`; the ordinary per-GPU `nvidia_suspend()`/`nvidia_resume()` callbacks retain their `nv->gpu_id` dispatch for PCI DPM. The patch does not alter full/default depth behavior.
- Expanded `tests/test_hybrid_pm_channel_depth.py` to require the three channel-depth guards, preserve per-GPU dispatch, and apply each patch without fuzz. `tools/audit_r580_modeset_pm.py` now distinguishes aggregate channel depth from per-GPU DPM. Audit [`r580-channel-dpm-modeset-audit-v2`](../evidence/r580-channel-dpm-modeset-audit-v2-20261021.json), SHA-256 `eeb9d5f3fce062a9311aa4d7ca5e21967493625bf245828738d19c34eb774d56`, reports all three guards and both per-GPU callbacks present in each exact built source tree.

## Previous increment: single-owner shared UVM PM primitive (2026-10-21)

## Previous increment: R580 VMA PM admission and release-list serialization (2026-10-21)

- Both R580 private providers now gate `vm_ops->access` with a nonblocking read attempt on `nv_system_pm_lock`. While the provider PM transition owns the write lock, direct `access_process_vm` access to the GPU mapping returns `-EAGAIN`; otherwise the wrapper calls the original implementation and returns its result unchanged. The pinned Linux v7.0 `vm_operations_struct` documents `.access` as the special-VMA fallback used by `access_process_vm`.
- `nv_alloc_release()` now holds the already initialized per-file `fp_lock` across the usage-count decrement and insertion into `free_list`. This prevents concurrent VMA close callbacks from overwriting each other’s list links. A compiled C/UBSan pthread test ran 48 distinct concurrent releases plus two concurrent releases of one shared allocation and verified every allocation was queued exactly once.
- R580 VMA `.open` remains an atomic mapping-reference increment. `.close` only decrements the allocation usage count and defers resources to the per-file free list; RM teardown remains in file release under the PM read lock. These callback semantics were source-audited; only the list operation receives the new lock.

Recorded inputs were unchanged; both builds report `loaded_or_installed=false`, `hardware_validated=false`, `runtime_isolation_validated=false`.

## Previous increment: R580 control-file PM lifetime and mmap admission (2026-10-21)

- Both private R580 variants now take the provider PM read lock while opening the shared control device. If that admission fails, the file-private allocation, stack object and `file->private_data` are all cleaned before returning the error.
- Control-device release now clears `file->private_data` before teardown, holds the provider PM read lock during RM/allocation cleanup, and queues an interrupted release to a worker that retains only `nv_linux_file_private_t`; it never dereferences the VFS `struct file` after release returns. This mirrors the existing GPU-file deferred-close lifetime model. The new CPU model exercises the synchronous control open/close path; source tests check the deferred callback's lock and file-independent signature.
- The native `.mmap` entry now uses a nonblocking PM read-trylock wrapper and returns `-EAGAIN` while the provider transition owns the write lock. VMA `.open`, `.close`, `.access` and later fault callbacks are still a separate unresolved lifetime boundary; this increment does not claim to drain them.

## Previous increment: R580 channels-only system-PM phase (2026-10-21)

- Both R580 backends now implement the full opt-in `channels` depth: suspend preempts only that provider's channels, skips UVM and GPU power transition; resume skips device resume and UVM resume, then restores channels. Error rollback tracks whether UVM suspend completed, collects provider/device/channel cleanup errors, and returns `NV_ERR_GENERIC` if rollback itself fails. In the pinned R580 UVM implementation, `uvm_suspend()` takes the global PM and VA-space write locks, drains fault/ISR work and returns `NV_OK`; Linux PCI DPM still owns physical-device power transitions.
- The proprietary `nv.c` patch is pinned to the fully composed private source tree and passes the builder's zero-offset provenance check. A first regeneration against clean vendor sources applied with a +2389-line offset and was rejected by the strict builder; that failed report/log is retained. ## Previous increment: provider-routed UVM CSL contexts (2026-10-21)

## Previous increment: UVM duplication and cross-provider P2P lifecycle (2026-10-21)

- Retained-channel references now match the pinned R580 contract: every successful retain increments the same pointer's reference count and each release reaches the owning provider; the address-space dependency persists until the final release. CPU/UBSan cases exercise overflow and owner conflicts.
- The frontend now routes `DupAddressSpace` and `DupMemory`/`FreeDupedHandle` through the tracked device owner. Duplicated address spaces are registered beneath their device; duplicate physical-memory handles remain typed Device children until provider release succeeds. The latest routes stage outputs and quarantine ambiguous backend results or identity collisions.
- Added `nvUvmInterfaceDupAllocation`: source and destination VA-space identities are both resolved before dispatch, the request goes only to the common owning RM, cross-provider mappings return `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED`, and failed outputs are withheld. Pinned R580 documents that vidmem duplication across GPUs is unsupported; Open R595's UVM call site currently asserts system-memory allocations for this mapping path.
- R580 UVM queries `GetP2PCaps` only after GPU registration, so the frontend's mixed-provider `UVM_LINK_TYPE_NONE` response cannot admit the second provider. Ordinary-mode initialization first maps existing semaphore-pool pages through `DupAllocation`; the current bridge rejects that cross-provider VA-space pair and UVM aborts GPU initialization. See the hash-pinned static trace in [the cross-provider audit](../evidence/r580-uvm-cross-provider-static-v2-20261023T204103Z.json). Runtime and hardware behavior remain unvalidated.
- `P2pObjectCreate/Destroy` now routes same-provider pairs with a provider-independent public `NvHandle`, translating it to the selected RM's native handle on destruction. Registry entries retain both device parents and the logical session. Cross-provider creation is rejected before RM dispatch; partial outputs and native-handle collisions reserve both parents in quarantine.

## Previous increment: UVM session ownership and global PM lifecycle audit (2026-10-21)

- Added `src/hybrid580_uvm_owner_map.h`: typed provider-owned identities, generation-checked references, same-provider multi-handle pinning, and separate `begin_retire`/`finish_retire` phases. Begin-retire closes new use before backend destruction; finish-retire removes the identity only after confirmed success. A cleanup pin is restricted to one quiescent retiring leaf. Its caller must externally serialize map operations; C references are ownership tokens and must not be copied or forged.
- Added `src/hybrid580_uvm_session_pair.h`: one logical UVM session backed by Open and proprietary child sessions. It publishes an identity only after both create calls succeed; partial create rolls back in reverse order. Explicitly retryable destruction may be retried; ambiguous destruction quarantines the handle and never dispatches it again. The composed CPU test confirms the pair and owner map preserve distinct native handles and that failed publication does not corrupt an existing identity.
- Focused source/CPU/UBSan tests pass: owner-map lifecycle/ABA/forgery cases, pair create/destroy rollback, composed session-owner contract, and two callback-PM audit cases. These primitives remain disconnected from the kernel frontend and all76 provider exports.
- `tools/audit_uvm_pm_lifecycle.py` checks clean Open and patched proprietary R580.178.04 trees. Report [`uvm-callback-pm-lifecycle-v2`](../evidence/uvm-callback-pm-lifecycle-v2-20261021.json), SHA-256 `92ce8ea3db32d8a279cba088cfb7b45b48fa6b84d8c1ed8b7869d5fd579b229c`, mode0600. It finds one callback slot per RM, a clear-then-ISR-quiesce unregister sequence, no UUID on global suspend/resume, local channel preemption before each provider's UVM suspend, and UVM's global PM write lock held across return until resume.
- The full suite passes **528 tests, zero skipped in31.462s**, with both probe environment variables pinned to `build/probe-phase-v1-20261021/hybrid_cuda_probe`: [`v4 log`](../evidence/logs/software-verification-uvm-primitives-v4-20261021.log), SHA-256 `f4d91766b8fc95caf657da892bdbc1ffbd87a5ae444a75d0355942aaee19b472`, mode0600.
- **Unresolved before callback fan-in:** cross-provider system-PM ordering. A first provider's callback can enter global UVM suspend while the other provider's channels remain active; a second callback can re-enter UVM's held PM lock. Resume also occurs before each provider restores its channels. The `GetP2PCaps`/P2P-object source route now preserves no-link discovery but has not executed through UVM or RM. Settle the PM barrier before wiring callback fan-in. The immediate next source audit is the PMA and paging pointer/lifetime cluster. The V100 remains in guest `pc3-v100`; RTX5080 remains on the PC3 host. No experimental module load, PCI rebind, replacement or reboot has occurred. Same-Linux same-process CUDA remains unachieved.

## Previous increment: class-sized `NVOS64` allocation copies (2026-10-21)

- The prior hypothesis that a non-null `pAllocParms` with outer `paramsSize=0` was an undeclared read is falsified. Both fresh PC3 installed-driver context captures show exactly that shape for `NV2081_BINAPI`, while the fixed four-byte class payload is present, reserved-zero, stable, and the allocation succeeds. The v2 paired report adds outer `paramsSize`; it finds zero on both branches, matching typed fields, and explicitly leaves same-kernel hybrid validation false: [`comparison`](../evidence/nv2081-binapi-allocation-comparison-v2-20261021.json), SHA-256 `ddda5cb47c8cbac129103970c6f9ef463a13fcebe233963e503fcfe494e59928`.
- Pinned Open R580 source explains this behavior. `entry_points.c` forwards the outer `paramsSize`, but `serverAllocApiCopyIn()` initializes a separate RM parameter-copy size; `rmapiParamsCopyInit()` derives that size from `rmapiGetClassAllocParamSize()` and the resource descriptor for the external class. The `NV2081_BINAPI` descriptor marks the four-byte payload optional. Hardware observation is the direct evidence for caller shape; source analysis is for the Open R580 copy path and does not establish proprietary internals.
- The strict size/pointer-pairing candidate was built and CPU-tested under the incorrect hypothesis, then rejected as soon as the actual traces were aggregated. It was never installed or loaded. Preserve its report/module/log as a negative experiment only; do not use it as the current source or qualification. The current frontend is restored to class-sized copy semantics. The lifecycle CPU scenario now covers size-zero/pointer-present success, a nested user-copy fault that stops before backend dispatch, optional absence, and frees all three constructed BINAPI children.
- Comparator v2 and all seven focused tests pass. The source/capture evidence manifest is [`nvos64-class-allocation-copy-contract-v1`](../evidence/nvos64-class-allocation-copy-contract-v1-20261021.json), SHA-256 `99384e38cc9d159b8ca9566f0257ed6cde78cf9d53787df0466cebdb21c424e5`.

## Previous increment: BINAPI association and REA/Ghidra setup (2026-10-21)

- Ordered, successful RM_ALLOC/RM_FREE headers now correlate the observed `0x20810108` control to a live `NV2081_BINAPI` object on both installed reference paths: V100/R580.178.04 and RTX5080/R595.99.02. Both report 992 outer bytes, flags0 and transport status0; nested payloads and pointers remain unread. Correlation evidence is `evidence/binapi-v100-object-correlation-v2-20261021.json` (SHA-256 `c71d994fbd86b4ed822fb3fa81b12530ce17b673a36dc4f14636eca4f845b43d`) and `evidence/binapi-rtx5080-object-correlation-v2-20261021.json` (SHA-256 `b48b03fac79f2c4d46d1b557972fe12659802be34ddd1711bee6c6221ff6f4b`). The traces come from separate Linux kernels.
- Compiled SDK facts and the source comparison confirm that `NV2081_BINAPI` aliases generic `FINN_RM_API`, while neither pinned public SDK declares a message-08 structure or contains the observed command literal. The class is registered internally as `BinaryApi`, parented to `Subdevice`, with optional four-byte allocation parameters and RPC/GSP allocation flags in both R580 and R595 source. Those allocation flags do not prove the 992-byte control is forwarded: the actual source `binapiControl_IMPL` only calls physical RM under its firmware-client predicate and otherwise returns its initialized `NV_OK`. The 32-byte generic FINN header is not the observed 992-byte packet, the outer serialized flag is clear, and the nested pointer contract is unresolved. Current v4 boundary report: `evidence/binapi-control-boundary-v4-20261021.json`, SHA-256 `d333e36115f79f71765cbe61e1ab550c1659e055aef03d11277f0bceae0a9ba7`; v3 is retained as superseded.

Marker searches found no BINAPI/FINN/20810108/NV2081 names; this is bounded string/symbol evidence, not proof of absent runtime metadata. Report: `evidence/pc3-rtx5080-libcuda-static-inspection-v1-20261021.json`, SHA-256 `889a408978b08a912817ee4678ac12accf94674aa86fbe61c11920d224b4cc92`.

GNU `objdump` evidence identifies the local R580 proprietary RM core as ELF64 x86-64 ET_REL, unchanged before/after at SHA-256 `5269a23ffba19a17fe16f076463e7ad4151254659441d1711bee6c6221ff6f4b`; Evidence IDs and bounded section/file-header results are retained in `evidence/rea-private-nv-kernel-*-v1-20261021.json`. Ghidra 12.1.4 and Ubuntu JDK21.0.12.1 were extracted under `build/tools/` from checksum-verified archives; no system Java, Codex, REA, or GPU driver configuration was changed. REA's provider check is healthy when these local paths are supplied; its overall environment check still reports unrelated, pre-existing client-registration drift, which was left untouched.

- The pinned-source registration assertions were added test-first (focused test failed on the absent report field, then passed after parsing R580/R595 `resource_list.h`); the full v87 suite passed at that checkpoint.

REA xrefs returns four source addresses, but each lies outside the ELF's `.text/.data/.rodata/.bss` virtual ranges; the ELF relocation table has no row naming this symbol. ## Objective, authority and qualification

One Linux host, V100 and Blackwell, one CUDA Driver API process with per-device contexts and correct numerical kernels. **Not achieved.** Modernization and performance gains require separate measured evidence.

- The user authorizes reference tests on both PC3 GPUs. PC3 runs kernel `7.0.0-38-generic` with RTX5080 under installed595.99.02. Its Tesla V100 SXM2 at host BDF `0000:81:00.0` remains assigned to `vfio-pci` for the active V100 VM; the guest is `pc3-v100`, kernel `6.8.0-147-generic`, driver `580.178.04`, and reports the V100 at guest BDF `0000:00:05.0`. Strict two-hop SSH now works through PC3 using the existing root-only guest key and pinned ED25519 fingerprint. Fresh V100 CUDA reference evidence: `evidence/pc3-v100-sm70-reference-v4-20261020.json`.
- The V100 SM70 numerical reference passed on the installed guest driver using CUDA Driver API13000. One V100 context ran the SM70 image for five vector sizes synchronously and asynchronously, with cleanup passing and memory use returning to0MiB. `hybrid_validated=false`: this is a V100-only reference in a separate guest, not same-Linux co-location or hybrid validation.
- No experimental module was installed or loaded, no operational driver was replaced, and the VM, PCI binding, services, boot state, firmware and Secure Boot were not changed during the installed-driver probes. Canary rollback remains unqualified for hardware use.
- NVIDIA's exact R580.178.04 product appendix lists Tesla V100 variants and RTX 5080, but its same-release kernel-module guide says Volta needs the proprietary flavor, Blackwell needs Open, and the flavors are mutually exclusive. This is package-level support, not a single stock module for both; it reinforces the custom same-version two-backend target. Official links and the design consequence are recorded in [architecture](ARCHITECTURE.md).
- The installed-driver RM_CONTROL captures contain 165 RTX5080/R595 and 170 V100/R580 fixed headers, with 58 common command IDs. Corrected analysis finds three real size differences and four same-size call-frequency differences; comparison v1 is preserved but superseded because its first version conflated the two. The observer did not read nested parameter payloads or addresses.
- A new C11 probe compiled the exact R580.178.04 and R595.99.02 SDK headers and independently reproduced the three parameter-size deltas. This is branch-level source/layout evidence, not a comparison between proprietary and Open R580 flavors and not a hardware or hybrid test.
- The fixed-header `RM_ALLOC` summary contains93 RTX and96 V100 records; all observed outer `paramsSize` values are zero. Each trace has a successful `NV01_DEVICE_0` (`0x80`) allocation under ROOT_CLIENT (`0x41`) with a non-null parameter pointer. No nested allocation bytes or pointer values were read. Pinned R580 source says `rmapiParamsCopyInit()` obtains copy size from class metadata, and `deviceConstruct_IMPL()` reads `NV0080_ALLOC_PARAMETERS.deviceId`; therefore outer `paramsSize=0` does not establish an empty class payload. The [v3 C11 SDK probe](../evidence/rm-control-layout-r580-r595-v3-20261021.json) measures a 56-byte `NV0080_ALLOC_PARAMETERS` with the same `deviceId` offset in R580/R595 and the 4-byte `GPU_GET_DEVICE_IDS` control structure. This narrows the next seam to device-instance translation plus child-handle ownership; it does not validate that route.
- The isolated RUSD observer reads only the typed eight-byte `polledDataMask`. Its build report confirms the R580.178.04 and R595.99.02 declarations are each eight bytes at offset0 with identical flag constants. On the installed RTX5080/R595 path the standard CUDA probe produced one successful `RM_USER_SHARED_DATA` allocation; entry/exit snapshots agree at mask0. On the installed V100/R580 guest path the same probe produced zero class allocations in a complete trace (zero payload snapshots). This says the observed default V100 CUDA path did not allocate RUSD; it does not rule out other V100 workloads or make the 5080 observation a hybrid result.
- The guest runner now verifies that the only accepted ED25519 fingerprint belongs to the exact forwarded endpoint `[127.0.0.1]:22223`; its known-hosts file must be a root-owned regular file that is not group/world-writable. A real-key regression test reproduces an unrelated-host fingerprint and proves it cannot satisfy the guest pin. The runner was exercised again on the V100 guest; SM70 reference passed and all temporary payloads were hash-checked and removed. No driver, module, VM topology, PCI binding, or boot state changed.
- Previous software verification: **385 tests passed, zero skipped in 28.520s** with both probe environment variables pinned to `build/probe-managed-prefetch-v2/hybrid_cuda_probe`; log `evidence/test-suite-map-memory-v80-20261021.log`, SHA-256 `0c8766a34a25e05a51119dbe14bfa5f622205ada27dcce273d2d1fdca27fda79`. The v78 stale-import assertion was corrected in v80. The following v82 suite run preserved a harness link error for the new legacy-only P2P symbols; v83 adds faithful CPU stubs and exercises the new routes.
- The first full-suite run without the required probe pins selected an obsolete `build/hybrid_cuda_probe` and failed16 probe-contract cases; `software-verification-v50` retains that result. The rerun with explicit current probe pins passed262 tests (v51); after the V100 cross-check and hardware-ID fixtures, v52 passed264.

## Next executable source increment

Continue the Linux PM/vector audit for the two private R580 backends. The inspected R580 Open source registers one threaded legacy/MSI vector on `nv->interrupt_line`, or `nvl->num_intr` threaded MSI-X vectors using `nvidia_isr_msix` and `nvidia_isr_msix_kthread_bh`. `nvidia_suspend()` holds `nvl->ldata_lock` across NVKMS suspend and `nv_power_management()`; the RM software interrupt gate occurs inside `rm_power_management()`, and `bottom_half_q` is stopped only after that call returns. The MSI-X wrappers use separate spinlock/mutex serialization. Determine whether a provider API can close all active ISR threads after aggregate channel preemption without deadlocking or disabling unrelated shared IRQ users, and how it must pair with the RM gate and UVM drain. Model the failure and resume paths before implementing the API. The existing aggregate PM coordinator and provider adapters remain unregistered. After this barrier is designed and reviewed, continue typed 76-entry routing and the remaining `NV0080_ALLOC_PARAMETERS` policy audit. Keep experimental modules unloaded; no same-kernel V100+Blackwell CUDA claim exists.

## Selected architecture and decisive unknowns

**Architecture C: separate same-version R580.178.04 backends**, proprietary physical Volta RM/wrapper under its original NVIDIA license and Open Blackwell RM/wrapper under Dual MIT/GPL, connected by a separate frontend. NVIDIA's product list includes both cards but its module guide confirms stock flavors are mutually exclusive; this dual-backend layer remains custom. A restricted custom BDF-to-GPU-file route now compiles against both backends. Open R580 retains Volta UVM source; three examined wrapper interfaces (`nv.h`, `nv-gpu-info.h`, `nv_uvm_interface.h`) match between flavors. This is partial source/layout evidence, not proprietary binary ABI or runtime compatibility proof.

The combined RM ET_REL object is a symbol-isolation experiment only. The Open wrapper has104 GPL-only kernel imports; do not relabel a proprietary combined object. The proprietary baseline has297 imports, zero GPL-only, while retaining44,969 naked-return and1,758 frame-pointer warnings. The Open return-thunk rebuild does not fix that binary.

Architecture A remains a source experiment: modern Open generated tables omit physical GV100 dispatch and essential GR controls. The opt-in firmware-policy patch alone cannot restore physical initialization. `nv-kernel.o_binary` is an object filename, not proof that source is unavailable: complete Open RM builds it. Partial GV100/no-GSP code survives; do not infer either complete absence or complete support from it. No fake successful callbacks or modern default HAL entries are acceptable.

Critical unresolved boundaries:

1. Both numeric-ID directions pass in one-shot tests and separate live co-lifecycle runs. A V100/R580 ROOT was mirrored and freed on RTX5080/R595 before freeing the V100 ROOT; the reverse order also passed. This proves simultaneous numeric-ID lifetimes across the installed R580 and R595 servers in both directions. It does not prove the target same-version R580 proprietary/Open pair, private-session cleanup, shared device files, or CUDA execution by experimental backends.
2. Installed traces and source builds prove three R580↔R595 fixed-header size differences. The R580 source rejects mismatched parameter sizes. This forbids blind cross-release forwarding, but the intended custom pair is same-version R580; no packet translation should be inferred from these cross-release captures.
3. Actual private VFS open/close, LSM, deferred fput, module references and RM cleanup have not run in the target kernel.
4. Both private wrapper/session adapters and the frontend compile. Per-open inventory snapshots merge into a BDF-sorted catalog; typed GPU metadata, device-instance masks, `NV01_DEVICE_0`, memory, VASpace, channel-group, context-share and GPFIFO channel allocation are routed through tracked backend handles. GPU-file map/`.mmap` and native `.poll` callbacks now compile and pass CPU ownership/lifetime models; stock R580 and R595 captures independently show map/mmap on a common per-generation GPU file identity. Actual frontend VFS open/close, descriptor installation, RM execution, page faults, event registration/wakeup, general control, UVM and deferred teardown remain untested. The private descriptor is still not standard CUDA device enumeration or CUDA compatibility.
5. Distinct endpoint identity tokens do not prove independent initialized RM global state. Distinct function pointers into one stock core cannot provide it.
6. Same-kernel V100/Blackwell hardware, recovery and controlled performance tests remain unavailable/unqualified.

## Implemented seams and invariants

### Transaction broker

`src/rm_root_client.c/.h`, typed raw bindings and `src/rm_session_endpoint.h` implement ROOT/ROOT_CLIENT allocation with real NVOS21 and NVOS64 layouts and reverse cleanup. Raw API remains a compile/link experiment. New opaque allocation entry points accept versioned invocation/context/backend/file identities, copy both descriptors before any callback and retain no raw stack/control/nvfp. The common callback may serve distinct sessions. Reject malformed/aliased identities before forwarding.

Publication requires both transport and embedded statuses. The prototype mirrors the first confirmed root ID into the second allocation using nonzero `hRoot`, consistent with the pinned R580 `RS_COMPATABILITY_MODE=1` source audit. Both installed-driver directions accepted the other branch's observed ID and freed the result, but neither source allocation remained live and the tests did not run the experimental private R580 pair. End-to-end dual-session ownership remains unproven. Reverse rollback frees confirmed allocations only. Ambiguous allocations and failed frees quarantine the record, even failure+zero ID; never blindly retry/free ambiguous outputs. NVOS64 requires null rights/parameters and flags NONE. Exact SDK status offsets are preserved.

Caller must serialize the whole transaction with its own frontend file/session mutex and hold backend/session references through cleanup or teardown, including quarantine. Broker does not pin references itself. Per-call stack and PM lock are supplied by each opaque callback; never retain a temporary stack or hold PM through a client lifetime.86 CPU scenarios =35 raw+51 opaque across both packet forms, UBSan; real RM is not executed. Latest bridge contains six entry definitions, still411 unique undefined names and no new dependency. Kbuild report compiles two real kernel objects without producing a module or running RM.

### Private Open backend patches0002–0008

- Private module/export namespace preserves export flavors. Exact read-only, NULL-default `hybrid_gpu_pci` selects one lowercase BDF; probe/count reject before BAR/RM effects.19 extracted-source CPU cases.
- Dynamic character major reserves256 minors, regular0–247/control255; error unwind and exact fops/major/minor validation. No public major195/autoload alias. Fixed truncated20-byte procfs buffer; private path requires22 bytes including NUL.
- Namespaced caps/NVLink/NVSwitch/IMEX resources;4324 table paths verified unique with real readers. NVSwitch/platform registration and public PCI/OF autoload tables removed. RM/caps/NVLink/IMEX global init still occurs before the PCI gate; allowed ARM64 PCI devices may create subordinate Tegra devices. No complete platform isolation claim.
- Versioned projection provider returns one reference to a genuine backend file containing embedded nvfp; no fake sidecar. Immutable copied operations, rwlock reader/writer registry, temporary callback module pin, writer waits for readers. Atomic O(1) callback must not sleep, invoke RM/fget or recurse. Direct backend path bypasses projection.38 CPU scenarios; no real provider/frontend registered.

### New patch0009: exclusive backend control session

`patches/r580.178.04/0009-private-open-control-session.patch` implements create/get/put/file APIs with kref, mutex and genuine backend struct file. Construction holds an anchor reference, validates its superblock/mount, allocates a private character inode and pseudo-file with pinned exact NVIDIA fops, then invokes actual `nvidia_open` to obtain the real embedded file-private/event container. No user fd is installed, no GPU file opened. Allocation failures unwind exactly once.

`alloc_file_pseudo` already sets FMODE_OPENED: a failed nvidia_open followed by fput can call release. An early NULL-private-data guard in nvidia_close handles incomplete construction; do not clear FMODE_OPENED to bypass VFS cleanup.

Invocation accepts only bounded kernel-owned root alloc21/64/free packets, writes a typed status sentinel, serializes the session, validates the genuine control file, takes PM read lock interruptibly, obtains a fresh stack, checks GPU state and calls real rm_ioctl. Each invocation releases its own stack/PM/mutex. Existing current-credential behavior is preserved; no worker or privilege override. No general user-pointer/nested-buffer translation is provided. Final reference does fput outside invocation locks; real RM cleanup can be deferred. The file accessor returns a held reference without sleeping for projection use; frontend unpublication/close synchronization is still the caller's responsibility.

54 CPU scenarios execute extracted helpers/vendor control-open and early failure prefixes with VFS/PM/RM substitutes and pthread serialization. They do not validate Linux LSM, deferred fput, IRQ/lockdep/module unload or real hardware. Upstream v7.0 VFS source was inspected; equivalence to Ubuntu's target implementation is not asserted. `pahole` unavailable; no measured kernel DWARF layout claim.

The first full build failed because wrapper and SDK rs_access.h files use separate pragma-once identities. Builder now overlays the **unchanged SDK nvos.h** beside wrapper headers in the scratch build, reusing the wrapper declarations. Negative compile reproduces the conflict, positive compile and full v2 build pass. Source bytes/notices preserved; shared endpoint header is also copied verbatim. Failed report `evidence/private-open-control-session-20261019.json` is retained.

## Cross-backend attach increment (2026-10-21)

- Open source patch `patches/r580.178.04/0013-private-control-attach.patch` factors the native `NV_ESC_ATTACH_GPUS_TO_FD` pin/rollback helper and exports a typed kernel-buffer attach API. Proprietary patch `patches/r580.178.04/proprietary/0012-private-legacy-control-attach.patch` uses the correctly prefixed `hybrid580_legacy_backend_file` helper.
- The catalog planner preserves caller order/repeats, keeps zero placeholders in the frontend view, and excludes zero from backend-local lists, matching the pinned wrapper's skip-zero behavior. The frontend accepts direct size-encoded attach and `NV_ESC_IOCTL_XFER_CMD`; XFER requires the exact structure size and inner command. It constructs two clean candidate control sessions, attaches each nonempty partition and swaps the pair after both succeed. On a backend attach error it leaves the old pair selected, quarantines further stateful use, and drops candidates after releasing locks. The source and CPU models acknowledge that final `fput` can defer RM cleanup.

## Prepared canary and final qualification

`src/hybrid_frontend_canary.c`, `tools/build_frontend_canary.py`, `tests/test_frontend_canary.py`: production executable `build/experiments/frontend-canary-v3/hybrid_frontend_canary`, SHA `4670dbb7587f044b6a1192ffd5da9788b2ac1238dcc2eb16eed7141d9acb309e`,57176bytes, built for kernel7.0.0-38. Build/report `evidence/frontend-canary-v3-20261019.json`;6 tests/51 process scenarios with syscall doubles and independent review pass. The production binary has **never run**, even preflight/help.

Default preflight verifies exact distinct BDFs, private module parameters and PCI driver/module links, kernel release, enable flag and misc identity without opening the frontend. Explicit `--execute` additionally checks node identity before/after open, allocates two clients in21/64 forms, verifies statuses/canonical class/distinct IDs, frees in reverse order with parent=root/0, and aborts on ambiguity with one close and no retry. It never loads drivers, opens official NVIDIA nodes, submits GPU work or manages a VM. No claim of fencing concurrent administrator changes or synchronous cleanup. Execution is not currently authorized on a prepared canary.

A test-isolation error initially left fortified `__readlink_chk` unwrapped: four local sysfs binding checks returned false before any control-device open. Exact paths/cause retained in the canary report; not evidence of PCI-device absence. The corrected fixture wraps fortified and ordinary entry points and refuses unwrapped OS symbols using nm before execution. A compiled-only negative witness reproduces that defect without running it. All production/runtime claims remain unvalidated.

The source, software and build work is reusable. The next discriminating result is real dual-RM/session/client behavior on initialized host hardware; writing a full generic router before that proof would assume the very ABI/lifetime property this prototype is meant to test. Milestone1 is implemented/compiled with CPU evidence; physical initialization, experimental CUDA, robustness and performance remain unqualified. No speedup measured or claimed.
