- `evidence/pc3-v100-guest-full-ioctl-trace-v2-20261020.json` and its `.strace.gz` sidecar are a fresh V100 guest run on kernel `6.8.0-147-generic`, R580.178.04, Driver API13000. SM70, five synchronous/asynchronous sizes and cleanup passed. The complete trace has607 ioctl lines /46,213 bytes, zero unparsed lines and zero negative syscall returns. The private sidecar is mode0600, raw SHA-256 `8e7d2a168be7b1b56e1e6fae71c9ed98faff4bbb1326e231cd99b4dd2d62c2dd`.
- Both traces cover `/dev/nvidiactl`, `/dev/nvidia0` and `/dev/nvidia-uvm`. The class-allocation-shaped request `0xc030462b` occurs100 times on RTX (93 control-node,7 GPU-node) and103 times on V100 (96 control-node,7 GPU-node); its encoded ioctl size is48 bytes. UVM ioctl counts are148 and147 respectively. Only the RTX trace contains request `0x46` (one call). The CUDA probe uses `cuMemAlloc` and pinned-host memory, not `cuMemAllocManaged`; UVM migration remains untested and the report explicitly records `uvm_workload_validated=false`.
- Reproducible, sidecar/hash-verified comparison: `evidence/pc3-volta-blackwell-full-ioctl-comparison-v1-20261020.json` (SHA-256 `233ba5ee2570a1067c3e34be75624666dc1de87a9731a27b1fb8fe70611f20ba`) generated by `tools/compare_ioctl_trace_evidence.py`. The normalized sequences share56 calls; their first difference is RTX `/dev/nvidia0` request `0xc00446c9` versus V100 `/dev/nvidiactl` request `0xc020462a`. The comparator does not retain ioctl arguments or claim payload statuses, file-object identity, UVM migration, or cross-backend compatibility.
- Static R595.99.02 source mapping: `0xc030462b` is `NV_ESC_RM_ALLOC` and the R595 validator accepts the48-byte NVOS64 form; `0xc00446c9` is `NV_ESC_REGISTER_FD`, whose public input contains `int ctl_fd`. R595 `escape.c` resolves that control fd, rejects self/cyclic linkage, then retains its file-private reference until the GPU file closes. Both live traces contain this request (RTX10 calls, V1009), but trace metadata does not reveal the fd value or prove that R580 implements identical internals. That trace comparison identified the frontend boundary. The BDF-selected GPU-file and same-backend `ctl_fd` source path now have CPU contracts, including held-reference cleanup; Linux VFS, the proprietary RM core and a target GPU remain untested.
- Static R595 UVM source maps request `0x46` to `UVM_PAGEABLE_MEM_ACCESS_ON_GPU`, a pageable-access query with its result in a hidden payload. One observed call is not a capability result and does not establish managed memory or migration. The two stacks are separate kernels; no same-process hybrid test follows.
- The first RTX trace attempt `evidence/pc3-rtx5080-full-ioctl-trace-v1-20261020.json` failed closed before launching the CUDA probe because Python's default source loader rejected the extensionless uploaded helper. `SourceFileLoader` now handles that transport name; a regression test exercises the extensionless path. The V100 guest loader uses the same robust path. Neither driver nor module was changed, and neither VM nor PCI binding was modified.

## Installed GPU-ID and attached-ID payloads (2026-10-21)

- The ptrace observer now reads only the fixed 32-byte `GPU_GET_ID_INFO_V2` and/or 128-byte `GPU_GET_ATTACHED_IDS` parameter block for explicitly selected commands. It records entry/exit values and status, omits pointer addresses, rejects invalid pointers, and never traces unrelated nested controls. The observer compiled twice byte-identically against the pinned R580 SDK: binary SHA-256 `e9f24e31e6b00035eb9515420e3e6d0f4ab77bbcd64e627dabcfe87e760e2157`, 84,024 bytes; full 205-file dependency closure in `evidence/rm-ioctl-observer-attached-ids-build-v2-20261021.json`.
- PC3 RTX5080/R595.99.02, kernel `7.0.0-38-generic`, Driver API13020: both calls returned transport and embedded status0. `GPU_GET_ID_INFO_V2` returned backend GPU ID512; `GPU_GET_ATTACHED_IDS` returned `[512, 0xffffffff, ...]`. Two observations of each command agree. The selected SM120 kernel passed architecture, all five synchronous and asynchronous vector lengths, and cleanup. Probe exits77 with `missing_sm=[70]`; this is a Blackwell-only installed-driver reference. Report `evidence/pc3-rtx5080-attached-ids-observation-v3-20261021.json`, SHA-256 `798d942f5dfb3a3f3693d7132fc593b188aa26a24033ce2bd94497222486cce9`.
- PC3 V100 guest/R580.178.04, kernel `6.8.0-147-generic`, Driver API13000: both calls returned transport and embedded status0 and both report GPU ID5; the attached table is `[5, 0xffffffff, ...]`. Two observations of each command agree. SM70 passed all five synchronous/asynchronous lengths and cleanup; GPU memory returned to0MiB. Report `evidence/pc3-v100-gpu-id-and-attached-ids-observation-v2-20261021.json`, SHA-256 `610846e29a0c7b4d82559bc59c964e9ec0b9f59f8cd85541bece1bb89a30f4b2`, mode0600.
- These captures show a shared fixed command/layout with branch-local GPU IDs (`5` on the V100 guest, `512` on RTX5080). They support the design requirement to map IDs through the BDF catalog; they do not prove one host can merge the two RM servers, do not observe `NV_ESC_ATTACH_GPUS_TO_FD`, and do not exercise the experimental frontend or CUDA across generations. Both installed driver stacks remain unchanged.
- Integration failures are retained: RTX report v1 stops at the acceptance runner's missing `--attached-ids` option before launching CUDA; report v2 runs the SM120 kernel but uses the obsolete probe binary, which lacks required memory-mode fields and is rejected by validation. The runner now forwards and validates both metadata flags, V100 defaults point to the current pinned probe, and report v3 is the accepted RTX reference.

## Next executable experiment

1. The full installed-driver V100/R580 bundle has passed on the VM guest; keep this as the V100 software/toolchain baseline and rerun only after relevant bundle changes or when extending coverage. Cooling confirmation was obtained before the run; do not infer it from telemetry alone.
2. **The same-Linux hybrid acceptance remains open.** The V100 remains in guest `pc3-v100` while the RTX5080 is on the PC3 host, so there is no same-process V100+Blackwell result. Build and review a host-specific recovery/rollback procedure before any topology change, experimental module load or installed-driver replacement.
3. The combined device-instance map is now wired into typed device allocation. Channel and parent handles are translated only after backend/class/ownership checks; state is retained for root-close cleanup and quarantined when RM transport, status, returned handle or output-copy results are ambiguous. Channel flags now accept only physical channel type plus runqueue values 0 or ONE; current installed-driver captures contain only flags0 on R580/V100 and flags0/16 on R595/RTX5080. A negative CPU scenario showed the old code forwarded `SKIP_MAP_REFCOUNTING` (bit3); it now rejects before the backend call. The supported runqueue-one flag is SDK field bits4:4, observed as `0x10` on RTX5080. See the fresh build and suite evidence above. This remains compiled/source/CPU evidence.
4. Selected-device MAP_MEMORY/UNMAP_MEMORY, mmap and poll now delegate through typed backend APIs to the BDF-selected native file. The map packet translates frontend handles into one backend namespace and reserves one target file binding; mmap consumes that binding and transfers VMA ownership to the native file. CPU/UBSan tests cover embedded RM failure, transport uncertainty, copyout fault, retry after mmap error, control/GPU targets, and file lifetime. Installed-driver map-to-mmap captures remain separate R580/V100 and R595/RTX5080 references; they do not execute this frontend. Next boundary: real VFS/RM execution, page faults/VMA split/close, event waitqueue behavior, standard NVIDIA node enumeration, remaining CUDA controls, and the newly inventoried 76-entry dual-provider UVM bridge.
5. The required same-Linux V100+Blackwell test still needs both GPUs visible to one kernel. The V100 remains assigned to the active `pc3-v100` VM while RTX5080 stays on the PC3 host. No stop/rebind, experimental module load, driver replacement or reboot was performed. Prepare the host-specific canary recovery and VMA/RM lifecycle evidence before proposing such a load. Until one process runs correct kernels on both GPUs, the primary objective remains unachieved; UVM, P2P, concurrency, prolonged stability and performance are also open.

## GPU map-context and VMA file identity (2026-10-21)

- The frontend now exposes a narrow GPU-file `.mmap` callback. A captured R580 `NVOS33+fd` map request is projected to the selected backend GPU file so the native RM attaches mapping context to that file. The frontend `.mmap` obtains the same backend file, uses exported Linux `vma_set_file()` before calling the native backend mmap handler, and drops only its temporary file reference. The VMA then owns the backend file across frontend-fd close. This relies on inspected R580 Open and R595 source ownership shape; it does not claim other mapping modes or CUDA allocation integration.

## Typed RM map/unmap and mmap binding (2026-10-21)

- The common frontend now routes `NV_ESC_RM_MAP_MEMORY` and `NV_ESC_RM_UNMAP_MEMORY` only through the backend that owns the active ROOT, device/subdevice and memory handles. Frontend handles are translated to backend-local handles; a target control or GPU frontend descriptor must be live, and a GPU target must belong to the originating control descriptor and selected backend.
- Each target frontend open-file has a single pending-map state: `EMPTY -> RESERVED -> READY -> MAPPING -> CONSUMED`. Embedded RM rejection returns to `EMPTY`; transport ambiguity and successful RM map followed by userspace copyout failure poison the target and quarantine the originating control session. Native mmap failure restores `READY` so the exact request can be retried. A consumed descriptor does not accept another map; open another target descriptor. `UNMAP_MEMORY` is typed and handle-translated, but cannot currently identify/recycle a target binding because its fixed packet has no fd.
- R580.178.04 `escape.c` confirms the native semantics: `NV_ESC_RM_MAP_MEMORY` is control-file-only, applies native security checks, calls `Nv04MapMemoryWithSecInfo`, installs the mmap context on `pApi->fd`, and rolls the mapping back if context installation fails. `osapi.c` requires the ROOT client process to match the current process. The frontend invokes that native path with a kernel-owned fixed packet and keeps transport status separate from the embedded `NVOS33` status.
- Backend build reports: Open `evidence/private-open-map-memory-v1-20261021.json` (report SHA-256 `a72f9ebff97885c565e93e7214efe1eaa20ac84dc699a38da81aa66748432505`; module SHA-256 `8ff2c7e0e2a7934b58f8f6ddfe44a6f3d7f2ea03f54803c0317be99bb3d3cba2`, 37,004,656 bytes,126 exports); proprietary `evidence/private-proprietary-map-memory-v3-20261021.json` (report SHA-256 `e04d3d226ffd04f388496d7fa191fd335076c0984c1a4c06c5495942202cde84`; module SHA-256 `fcdcd247e05067caf372edf77f6cc692c6c91b9f7beacc8123f5e66eef60425c`,136,663,680 bytes,127 exports,303 imports,zero GPL-only imports). The Open build retains baseline objtool output (10,857 naked-return/return-thunk lines; full-kernel mitigation qualification false); the proprietary binary-core build retains 44,969 naked-return and1,758 frame-pointer warnings. No warnings were suppressed.
- Frontend default inputs now select these exact map-capable backend trees. Build `evidence/frontend-map-memory-v3-20261021.json` (report SHA-256 `ef1fbb0f4b3d46355e17eb86a9918c3f0f62eaa00800cbeb57affd004f8745cd`; module SHA-256 `aaa54efe3fe5e0d6657303f4fec04548b4b1e6fde247bdaf3ba547fe80fceeca`,963,264 bytes) resolves all expected imports, including both backend map/unmap symbols; no unresolved imports. It retains one pahole-version warning.
- The frontend CPU/UBSan suite now includes embedded-status retry, transport-failure poison, copyout-failure poison and control-file mmap retry after backend error. Full suite v80 passed385 tests with no skips. None of these builds or CPU models loaded a module or exercised Linux VFS, NVIDIA RM/GPU hardware, standard `libcuda`, UVM, P2P or same-process cross-generation CUDA. The primary objective remains unachieved.

## NVOS56 mapping-update route and full R595 backend (2026-10-23)

- Source inspection across the pinned R580.178.04, R595.99.02 and R615.71.10 trees confirms the same `NVOS56_PARAMETERS` layout and `NV_ESC_RM_UPDATE_DEVICE_MAPPING_INFO` value/dispatch. R580/R595 document the address fields as scalar mapping keys; the provider must preserve their bits and call RM synchronously in the originating process. This is source evidence, not a proprietary-RM equivalence claim.
- `src/hybrid_frontend.c` routes this fixed ioctl only after validating the active ROOT, supported memory/channel class, same backend and matching physical `NV01_DEVICE_0` ancestry for the device and mapping object. It translates ROOT/device/memory handles into one backend namespace, preserves both `NvP64` values, and keeps embedded RM status separate from transport status. Ambiguous transport or a successful RM update followed by copyout failure quarantines the control session. Open and proprietary providers hold the session and PM locks and call their native escape inline.
- CPU regression scenarios cover malformed size, backend/class mismatch, same-backend cross-GPU ancestry, translated handles, high-bit scalar addresses, embedded error status and ambiguous transport. Full software suite passed **1,076 tests** with exit0: `evidence/full-suite-nvos56-v1-20261023T150618Z.json` (SHA-256 `9a6d215fad297e2b03143e05577b480d1459da6d1c594c128cf3dcf91b848a4e`); mode0600 log `evidence/logs/full-suite-nvos56-v1-20261023T150618Z.log` (SHA-256 `0138c61759b739d64901ecc8597614f19f2fb130d1d8038dd787e8a57dcce859`).
- The exact R580→R595 patch replay applied all39 patches without fuzz/offset; pinned source files and patch inputs stayed unchanged. Report `evidence/r595-r580-patch-port-v39-20261023.json` SHA-256 `0751216a27ae26c18c2aa7e3879fe2e68243ae7d8ea8cc89f401e118f03e90cc`. The isolated full R595.99.02 backend compiled for kernel7.0.0-38-generic: `hybrid580_open.ko`, 37,178,648 bytes, SHA-256 `2476c84c1ec355429a0361af1bd1bf23a7106fc2a6a1c7dc1773351a8d0bb07d`; report `evidence/r595-full-patched-open-build-v1-20261023.json` SHA-256 `b64377d98cee730faefc77740b8045ca81f43ddc52593f761ae9a0060bc6733a`.
